BackgroundJS199 / 226

Code rain

Columns of characters fall on a canvas; a translucent black fill each frame leaves fading trails.

Key idea
ctx.fillStyle = 'rgba(0,0,0,.08)'; ctx.fillRect(0, 0, w, h); // trails
ctx.fillText(char, x, drops[i] * size);
HTML
<div class="matrix" id="matrix">
  <canvas></canvas>
</div>
CSS
/* var(--violet), var(--ease-out)… are design tokens defined once in :root */

.matrix { position: absolute; inset: 0; background: #000; }

.matrix canvas { width: 100%; height: 100%; display: block; }
JavaScript
const PALETTE = ['#7c5cff', '#ff5c8a', '#22d3ee', '#fbbf24', '#34d399'];
const loops = [];
const addLoop = (el, fn) => loops.push({ el, fn });
const tickAll = t => {
  loops.forEach(l => { if (live(l.el)) l.fn(t); }); // skip off-screen canvases
  requestAnimationFrame(tickAll);
};
requestAnimationFrame(tickAll);
function setupCanvas(host) {
  const cv = $('canvas', host), ctx = cv.getContext('2d');
  const fit = () => {
    const dpr = Math.min(2, devicePixelRatio || 1);
    cv.width = host.clientWidth * dpr; cv.height = host.clientHeight * dpr;
    ctx.setTransform(dpr, 0, 0, dpr, 0, 0);
  };
  fit(); addEventListener('resize', fit);
  return { cv, ctx, w: () => host.clientWidth, h: () => host.clientHeight };
}

{
  const host = $('#matrix'), m = setupCanvas(host);
  const chars = 'アイウエオカキクケコサシスセソタチツテト0123456789ABCDEF', size = 14;
  let drops = [], last = 0;
  const reset = () => (drops = Array.from({ length: Math.ceil(m.w() / size) }, () => Math.random() * -20));
  reset(); addEventListener('resize', reset);
  addLoop(host, t => {
    if (t - last < 50) return; last = t;            // ~20fps is enough here
    const ctx = m.ctx;
    ctx.fillStyle = 'rgba(0,0,0,.09)'; ctx.fillRect(0, 0, m.w(), m.h()); // fading trails
    ctx.fillStyle = '#34d399'; ctx.font = `${size}px monospace`;
    drops.forEach((y, i) => {
      ctx.fillText(chars[Math.floor(Math.random() * chars.length)], i * size, y * size);
      drops[i] = (y * size > m.h() && Math.random() > .975) ? 0 : y + 1;
    });
  });
}
19

SVG & path animations

SVG lets you animate strokes, shapes and paths. The key trick: set pathLength="1", then stroke-dasharray: 1 and animate stroke-dashoffset from 1 to 0 to "draw" any line.

SVG200 / 226

Line drawing

Illustrations, signatures and logos that draw themselves. Each path is offset in time.

Key idea
path { stroke-dasharray: 1; stroke-dashoffset: 1; animation: draw 4s infinite; }
@keyframes draw { 45% { stroke-dashoffset: 0; } }
HTML

  <path class="stroke" d="M10 125 L70 50 L100 85 L140 30 L210 125 Z" pathlength="1"></path>
  <circle class="stroke s2" cx="185" cy="32" pathlength="1" r="14"></circle>
  <path class="stroke s3" d="M10 135 Q40 125 70 135 T130 135 T190 135 T215 133" pathlength="1"></path>
</svg>

20

3D transforms

CSS can place elements in real 3D space: perspective on the parent, transform-style: preserve-3d on the container, and rotateX/Y + translateZ on children. (See also the cube in principle 11 and the tilt card in the pointer section.)

JavaScript
{
  const card = $('#depthCard'), zone = card.parentElement;
  zone.addEventListener('pointermove', e => {
    const r = card.getBoundingClientRect();
    const x = clamp((e.clientX - r.left) / r.width - .5, -.6, .6), y = clamp((e.clientY - r.top) / r.height - .5, -.6, .6);
    card.classList.remove('auto');
    card.style.setProperty('--ry', x * 34 + 'deg'); card.style.setProperty('--rx', -y * 34 + 'deg');
  });
  zone.addEventListener('pointerleave', () => { card.style.setProperty('--rx', '0deg'); card.style.setProperty('--ry', '0deg'); setTimeout(() => card.classList.add('auto'), 400); });
}
3DJS219 / 226

Split-flap flip clock

Each digit has a top flap that falls (rotateX(-90deg)) and a bottom flap that lands, showing the real time.

Key idea
.go .ftop    { animation: flapTop .3s ease-in forwards; }
.go .fbottom { animation: flapBottom .3s ease-out .3s forwards; }
HTML
<div class="fclock" id="fclock"></div>
CSS
/* var(--violet), var(--ease-out)… are design tokens defined once in :root */

.fclock { display: flex; gap: 6px; align-items: center; }

.fclock .colon { font: 800 2rem var(--mono); color: var(--muted); padding: 0 2px; }
JavaScript
{
  const fc = $('#fclock'), digits = [];
  for (let i = 0; i < 6; i++) {
    if (i === 2 || i === 4) { const c = document.createElement('span'); c.className = 'colon'; c.textContent = ':'; fc.appendChild(c); }
    const d = document.createElement('div');
    d.className = 'fd';
    d.innerHTML = '<div class="half top"><span></span></div><div class="half bottom"><span></span></div><div class="flap ftop"><span></span></div><div class="flap fbottom"><span></span></div>';
    fc.appendChild(d); digits.push(d);
  }
  const setDigit = (d, v, animate) => {
    const old = d.dataset.v;
    if (old === v) return;
    const [top, bottom, ftop, fbottom] = $$('span', d);
    d.dataset.v = v;
    if (!animate || old === undefined) { top.textContent = bottom.textContent = ftop.textContent = fbottom.textContent = v; return; }
    top.textContent = v; bottom.textContent = old; ftop.textContent = old; fbottom.textContent = v;
    restart(d, 'go');
    clearTimeout(d._t);
    d._t = setTimeout(() => { bottom.textContent = v; ftop.textContent = v; d.classList.remove('go'); }, 650);
  };
  const tick = animate => {
    const n = new Date();
    const s = [n.getHours(), n.getMinutes(), n.getSeconds()].map(x => String(x).padStart(2, '0')).join('');
    digits.forEach((d, i) => setDigit(d, s[i], animate));
  };
  tick(false);
  setInterval(() => { if (!isPaused()) tick(live(fc)); }, 1000);
}

Cover flow carousel

Items left and right of the center turn away and sink back in Z; clicking any item brings it to the front.

Key idea
el.style.transform = `translateX(${off * 70}px) translateZ(${-Math.abs(off) * 90}px)
                      rotateY(${-Math.sign(off) * 45}deg)`;

  <i style="background:linear-gradient(135deg,#fbbf24,#f97316)">3</i>
  <i style="background:linear-gradient(135deg,#a855f7,#3b82f6)">4</i>

  <button class="ui-btn" id="cfNext">→</button>
</div>

/* var(--violet), var(--ease-out)… are design tokens defined once in :root */

.ui-btn {

  background: #262d3f;
  font-weight: 600;
  font-size: .85rem;

.ui-btn:hover { background: #2f3750; }

.dd > .ui-btn span { display: inline-block; transition: transform .3s var(--ease-out); }

.dd.open > .ui-btn span { transform: rotate(180deg); }

.cf {
  position: relative;
  width: 100%;
  height: 170px;
  perspective: 800px;
  transform-style: preserve-3d;
  margin-bottom: 30px;
}

.cf i {
  position: absolute;
  left: 50%;
  top: 14px;
  width: 110px;
  height: 140px;
  margin-left: -55px;
  border-radius: 12px;
  cursor: pointer;
  display: grid;
  place-items: center;
  font-style: normal;
  font-weight: 800;
  font-size: 1.3rem;
  transition: transform .6s var(--ease-out), filter .6s;
  box-shadow: 0 14px 30px -10px rgba(0, 0, 0, .8);
}

.cf-btns {
  position: absolute;
  bottom: 12px;
  left: 0;
  right: 0;
  display: flex;
  justify-content: center;
  gap: 8px;

JavaScript
{
  const cf = $('#coverflow'), items = $$('i', cf);
  let idx = 2;
  const lay = () => items.forEach((el, i) => {
    const off = i - idx;
    el.style.transform = `translateX(${off * 70}px) translateZ(${-Math.abs(off) * 90}px) rotateY(${-Math.sign(off) * 45}deg)`;
    el.style.zIndex = 10 - Math.abs(off);
    el.style.filter = off ? 'brightness(.6)' : 'none';
  });
  items.forEach((el, i) => el.addEventListener('click', () => { idx = i; lay(); }));
  $('#cfPrev').addEventListener('click', () => { idx = Math.max(0, idx - 1); lay(); });
  $('#cfNext').addEventListener('click', () => { idx = Math.min(items.length - 1, idx + 1); lay(); });
  lay();
}
21

Best practices

Motion is a tool, not decoration. Here's what separates polished product animation from distracting animation.

Performance

  • Animate transform and opacity — the browser composites them on the GPU without re-layout.
  • Avoid animating width, height, top, left, margin on big or many elements (they trigger layout every frame).
  • For a shadow change, fade in a pseudo-element's shadow with opacity instead of animating box-shadow.
  • Use will-change only on elements about to animate, never globally.
  • In JS, update inside requestAnimationFrame; read layout first, then write.

Timing cheat-sheet

  • 100–200ms — micro feedback: hover, press, toggle
  • 200–300ms — small: tooltip, dropdown, checkbox
  • 300–500ms — medium: modal, card expand, page section
  • 500–800ms — large or expressive: drawers, hero entrances
  • Exits ≈ 70–80% of the entrance duration
  • Stagger 30–80ms per item; cap the total so long lists don't drag

Accessibility

  • Respect prefers-reduced-motion: remove large movement, parallax and auto-play loops (keep simple fades).
  • Never flash more than 3 times per second (WCAG 2.3.1).
  • Auto-moving content longer than 5s needs a pause control (WCAG 2.2.2) — like the button at the top.
  • Don't communicate meaning with motion alone; keep visible focus states.
@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
  *, *::before, *::after {
    animation-duration: .01ms !important;
    animation-iteration-count: 1 !important;
    transition-duration: .01ms !important;
    scroll-behavior: auto !important;
  }
}

Purpose — avoid these

  • Animating everything at once: nothing stands out (breaks staging).
  • Making users wait for an animation before they can act.
  • Long intro animations on every visit.
  • Motion that shifts layout and moves what the user was about to click.
  • Linear easing on UI movement; bouncy easing in serious, data-heavy tools.
  • Every animation should have a job: feedback, orientation, focus, continuity or delight.

Tools & libraries

  • CSS transitions, keyframes, scroll-driven animations — most of this page.
  • Web Animations API — el.animate() with play / pause / reverse.
  • GSAP — timelines, ScrollTrigger, complex sequencing.
  • Motion (formerly Framer Motion) — declarative animation for React and JS, springs, layout animations.
  • Lottie / Rive — designer-made vector animations, interactive state machines.
  • Three.js — WebGL 3D scenes. Animate.css — ready-made classes like the entrance and attention tiles above.
